# Frontend overview

What the Angular app contains today and the details a change must keep. The rules are in `.claude/rules/frontend.md`, `testing.md` and `testing-frontend.md`; the HTTP shapes in `docs/api-contract.md`.

## Shell, routing, auth

- `src/index.html`: title, meta description, `<link rel="canonical">` and Open Graph tags (`og:url`, `og:image` `https://nfnn.cz/images/og-image.jpg` 1200×630 JPEG, a placeholder cut from the hero until real content arrives, `twitter:card`); `core/seo/canonical-link.ts` (`CanonicalLink.start()` from an app initializer, `SITE_ORIGIN` `https://nfnn.cz`) rewrites the canonical link and `og:url` to the path of every navigation without query or fragment; `public/robots.txt` keeps crawlers out of `/api/`, `/mcp`, `/darce/`, `/sprava/` and the link pages and names `public/sitemap.xml` (home and the privacy policy; news and project pages wait for a generated sitemap); icons (`favicon.svg` is the only linked tab icon, everything that can be SVG on the site is SVG, Jakub 2026-10-30; `favicon.ico`, linked from nowhere, answers services that ask only the default address `/favicon.ico`: without it claude.ai showed the DuckDNS duck for the connector, the icon of the parent domain; `apple-touch-icon.png`, which iOS takes only as PNG; `app-icon.svg`, linked from nowhere, is the icon for the ZimaOS app list, set there as its icon URL since 2026-10-30); loads nothing from other sites. Launch (#348): `<app-root>` holds a static launch screen (`.launch-screen`, `src/styles/_launch-screen.scss`: the theme's `icon-512.png` at `--launch-icon-size` in the middle of `--color-surface`), which the page paints before any script runs and Angular replaces with the first page; `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` (with `mobile-web-app-capable`, which keeps Chrome from warning) makes iOS fetch the `apple-touch-startup-image` links when the site is added to the Home Screen (iOS 27 fetched none without it), one per iPhone and iPad screen and orientation of `STARTUP_SCREENS` in `scripts/make-startup-images.mjs`, which the `postbuild` of `npm run build` draws into `startup/<width>x<height>.png` of the build from the built manifest's `background_color`, `icon-512.png` and the built stylesheet's `--launch-icon-size` (no library: it reads and writes the PNGs itself), so the launch image iOS shows at once equals the page's first paint and a theme brings its own; `scripts/make-startup-images.test.mjs` keeps the links of `index.html` equal to that list. The images are not in `ngsw-config.json`: iOS keeps its own copy from the moment of adding, so an installed app shows a changed launch image only after it is added again. `main.ts` bootstraps and references `@angular/localize`. `styles.scss` loads the theme, reset, keyframes; the scrollbars are the browser's own (Jakub 2026-11-05: no custom scrollbar styles).
- Zoom (Jakub 2026-10-31, plan v3 2.23d): the viewport meta in `src/index.html` is `width=device-width, initial-scale=1, maximum-scale=1, viewport-fit=cover` (Android Chrome and the desktop emulators then never pinch-zoom the page), `html { touch-action: manipulation }` in `styles.scss` stops double-tap zoom, and `core/zoom/page-pinch-lock.ts` (`PagePinchLock.start()` from the app initializer `pagePinchLockAtStart`) cancels Safari's `gesturestart`, `gesturechange` and `gestureend` on the document (non-passive), because Safari on the iPhone and iPad ignores the viewport limit; no `touchmove` listener, so scrolling never waits on script. The text size still follows the browser and the system (rem tokens); in the app opened from the home screen of an iPhone or iPad, which has no aA menu, `core/zoom/system-text-size.ts` (`SystemTextSize.start()` from the app initializer `systemTextSizeAtStart`) puts `follows-system-text-size` on `html` when `navigator.standalone` is `true` on a touch screen (a Mac's Dock app has none), and `styles.scss` gives that root `font: -apple-system-body` with `font-family: var(--font-sans)`, so the root size is the iPhone's text size setting (Dynamic Type, 17 px at the default; Jakub 2026-11-05, Q13); Safari in a tab and every other browser keep 16 px and their own text size. Playwright's WebKit has no Dynamic Type, so the size itself is a device check. Photos and the Přispět QR open in `shared/ui/zoom-viewer`. At the largest system text (53 px, AX5) the fluid heading tokens shrink to what the phone's width allows (`max(1rem, min(<rem>, 16vw))` as their minimum, and `--text-hero` `max(1rem, min(3.25rem, 27vw))`, so a long Czech word and the hero's "naděje" still fit a phone's line; unchanged at the default size from 320 px and at 200 % from 390 px), every text breaks a word only when it cannot fit (`overflow-wrap: break-word` on `html` in `styles.scss`, inherited by every text), the sign-in dialog's Přihlášení / Registrace switch stacks its buttons (`flex-wrap`), and a dialog's × keeps the size of its fixed button (`--text-dialog-close`); `e2e/largest-text.spec.ts` checks the header, the headings and the switch at 53 px on 320 and 375 px.
- Touch (#380, #385, 2026-11-06): a touch answers as in a native iOS app. `src/styles/_controls.scss` (loaded by `styles.scss` before the `ui-` blocks) removes Safari's grey tap flash (`-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` on `html`) and makes every control (`a[href]`, `button`, `summary`, `[role="button"]`, `[role="tab"]`) unselectable and undraggable, without the long-press callout (no link preview with "Otevřít / Přidat do seznamu četby", no "Zkopírovat / Vyhledat" over a label), and decorative images (`img[alt=""]`) and images inside controls without callout and drag; links to another site, `mailto:` and `tel:` keep the callout, so the address can still be copied or shared, and links without a class inside running text (`p` other than the crumbs, `dd`, `address`, `app-markdown`) stay selectable with the system menu, as all content text, the bank details and every field do. A pressed state on `:active` (`src/styles/_pressed.scss`: `when-pressed` for an enabled control, `look`, which under `forced-colors` becomes a 2 px `Highlight` outline inside the control): by default a tint of the control's own text colour (`--pressed-tint-amount` of `currentColor`, 8 % in `nova-nadeje`) laid as a background image over its background, with a weight (`:active:active` in `_controls.scss`) that wins over a component's base rule but loses to its own `:active` rule and to the `ui-` blocks; the tint's strength is the registered `--pressed-tint` (`@property` in `styles.scss`, a `<percentage>`, not inherited, `0%` at rest; a registered `<color>` holding `currentColor` does not interpolate in Chromium, which keeps `currentcolor` in its computed value) in the background image `linear-gradient(color-mix(in srgb, currentColor var(--pressed-tint), transparent), …)` that every control carries at rest (`pressed.fades-tint-back`, zero weight through `:where()`), so it comes at once on the press (its transition takes `0s` on `:active`, for that property alone) and fades back in `--duration-base` when the finger lifts; the `motion` mixins `state`, `hover` and `move` put `--pressed-tint` into their transition list for that, and a control whose own `background:` shorthand would reset the image sets `background-color` instead (`ui-list__row`, `ui-link-button`, `ui-pill-button`, `ui-cta`, `.ui-segmented button`, the account sheet, the sheet head's close button, the log reader's rows); a browser without `@property`, or a control whose own `transition` leaves the tint out, drops it at once as before; under `forced-colors` the tint stays `0%` and only the outline shows; `prefers-contrast: more` strengthens `--pressed-tint-amount` (14 % in `nova-nadeje`), and `check-themes.mjs` measures each text colour on its own tint; the solid `.ui-button` and `.ui-cta` darken (`--pressed-brightness`), the header's logo fades as a bar button of iOS does (`fades`: `--pressed-opacity`, 0.35 in `nova-nadeje`, at once on touch and back in `--duration-base`, with no tint, which drew a box around it), `.ui-cta--ghost` and the rows of `.ui-list` tint, a card that its link covers (news, featured news, project) shrinks (`--pressed-scale` in `--duration-fast`, back in its `--duration-base`; under `prefers-reduced-motion` it darkens instead) and a tab of the tab bar shows the pill (`--color-tab-pill`, whose label contrast `check-themes.mjs` already proves). Safari on the iPhone applies `:active` only while the document listens to touches, so `core/touch/pressed-on-touch.ts` (`PressedOnTouch.start()` from the app initializer `pressedOnTouchAtStart`) keeps a passive no-op `touchstart` listener on the document. Hover looks stand only inside `@media (hover: hover)`, so a tap leaves none behind (a card no longer stays lifted); the stylelint rule `nfnn/hover-only-with-hover` (`scripts/stylelint-hover-media.mjs`, run by `npm run lint:styles`, tested by `stylelint-hover-media.test.mjs`) refuses a `:hover` outside it. `e2e/touch-controls.spec.ts` checks what selecting a page takes and leaves; the callout and the pressed look are device checks, since Playwright's browsers know no `-webkit-touch-callout`.
- On an iPhone Safari 26 lets the page show under its status bar (Jakub 2026-11-05, replacing the blue strip of 2026-10-30). Safari 26 ignores `<meta name="theme-color">`, and the site sets none: WebKit (`LocalFrameView::fixedContainerEdges`) hit-tests the middle of the top edge 4 px inside the viewport, ignoring `pointer-events`, and walks up to the first fixed or sticky box at least 90 % as wide as the viewport (it skips one with `visibility: hidden`, opacity under 0.1, or 10 px or thinner). Without such a box, or when that box or anything on the way to it has a `backdrop-filter` (`PredominantColorType::Multiple`), it leaves the bar untinted with the page under it; otherwise it tints the bar with the first plain `background-color` on the way, and a fixed box without one is sampled once from the pixels under it and kept. The app header is `position: absolute` and the tab bar sits at the bottom, so no page has such a box at the top edge; a new fixed or sticky bar at the top keeps it that way with a `backdrop-filter` or changes the status bar with it. Chrome on Android shows its default toolbar. The app opened from the home screen of an iPhone or iPad asks for the same with `<meta name="apple-mobile-web-app-status-bar-style" content="black-translucent">` (Jakub 2026-11-06: the landing photo runs under the clock); without it iOS starts the web view under the status bar and paints the bar in the page's background. Two things follow there, both measured in the iOS 27.2 simulator and set only under `@media (display-mode: standalone)` with `@supports (-webkit-touch-callout: none)` in `styles.scss`, so a Safari tab, Android and a Mac's Dock app keep `env(safe-area-inset-top)` and `100svh`. WebKit gives `100svh`, `100%` and the root's `clientHeight` the screen less the status bar, and when the document is no taller than that it shrinks the whole viewport to it (`innerHeight` 894 of 956 on an iPhone 18 Pro Max), so the fixed tab bar of the landing page floated a status bar's height above the bottom over an empty band; there `--full-screen-height` (`100svh` in the theme) becomes `calc(100svh + env(safe-area-inset-top))` and `body` takes it as its `min-height`, so every page is at least as tall as what the app shows and the viewport keeps the whole screen. Not `100lvh` (#552 until 2026-11-12): iOS keeps the status bar mode an app was added with, so an app added before the page asked for `black-translucent` still shows a solid status bar, and there the viewport is 894 of 956 while `100lvh` stays 956, so the landing page scrolled by the status bar's height (Jakub's iPhone); `100svh` plus the top inset is 956 in the new mode (894 + 62) and 894 in the old one (894 + 0), measured on an iPhone 18 Pro Max in the iOS 27.2 simulator. Playwright has neither mode, so `e2e/first-screen.spec.ts` resolves the rule's expression with the two measured sets of viewport units and checks that it gives exactly the visible height in both. The landing page is one photo over the whole screen that never moves, in every setting (Jakub 2026-11-12): `features/content/landing/landing-stage.ts` (`holdLandingStage`, started by `Landing` after its first render) marks the root `landing-stage` while the hero fits one screen, which turns off the document's scrolling (`overflow: hidden` on `html` and `body`) and holds the page at `--page-resting-scroll`, scrolling it back there after anything moves it and dropping the stage, back at the top, once enlarged text makes the hero taller than the screen, after which the page scrolls as any page. A page in a Safari tab on an iPhone or iPad cannot draw under the status bar or under the floating toolbar; WebKit lays the page out under them only from the document's scrolled content, so in an iOS browser tab (`@media not (display-mode: standalone)` with `@supports (-webkit-touch-callout: none)`) the stage gives `body` `--landing-bleed-top` (120 px) of padding above and `--landing-bleed-bottom` (160 px) below, rests scrolled by the top one (and leaves the page where the browser put it while a dialog is open, so iOS can lift a field above the keyboard), and stretches the photo, its overlay (85 % shade held over the bottom bleed, so the text keeps its contrast) and its top shade over both, with the hero at least `100dvh` tall (`hero.scss`), so the photo runs under the clock and under the toolbar and the header keeps its place (`.app-header` sits at `top: var(--page-resting-scroll)`); The `body` background of the landing page could not do it: Safari paints the strips outside the page in the background colour only, never an image (measured in the iOS 27.2 simulator). Elsewhere (the home-screen app, a Mac, Android) both tokens are `0px` and the stage only stops the scrolling. `e2e/first-screen.spec.ts` checks on every screen size that the photo covers the screen and that the wheel and the keys move nothing, and, with the bleed of the iOS rule applied, that the photo reaches 62 px above and 98 px below the iPhone 18 Pro Max's Safari viewport, the status bar and toolbar measured there. And Safari blurs the top of the page under and just below the status bar (the scroll edge effect of iOS), which blurred the logo; there `--safe-area-top` (`env(safe-area-inset-top)` in the theme) adds `--home-screen-top-clearance` (16 px in `nova-nadeje`: the blur reaches about 25 pt below the status bar, measured with lines of text every 10 pt on an iPhone 18 Pro Max, and the header row centres the logo 14 px further down), and everything placed from the top safe area takes it: the header, the back button, `scroll-padding-top`, the hero, the page intros, the first sections of O nás and Přispět, `.ui-panel-page`, the 404 page, the donor area, the administration, the side panel and a full-screen dialog with its ×. iOS may keep the status bar mode of an app added before, until it is added again; the clearance keeps the content out of the blur in either mode. Playwright can neither open a home-screen app nor emulate `display-mode`, so `e2e/status-bar.spec.ts` checks only the meta, and the rest is a device check (the photo under the clock, the tab bar at the bottom of the landing page and of a short page, a sharp logo). `e2e/status-bar.spec.ts` checks the top edge at 390 px on `/`, `/projekty` and the privacy policy (opened and scrolled) and on the donor overview, and that the page paints no tint and names no theme colour. `app.ts` renders `<router-outlet />` and the sign-in dialog while `LoginDialogService.isOpen()` (Back closes it, and goes back a step first from the code from the app and from the recovery by payment, through `DialogHistory`; a sign-in with `returnTo` hands its entry over to that page, the privacy policy link in the registration replaces it) (re-created on every open so panels start clean, except a half-filled registration that `LoginDialogService` keeps without its password until a sign-in or a sent registration; its "Přihlášení / Registrace" switch carries `aria-pressed` and shows only on those two starting steps). `app.config.ts`: `provideRouter` with `chunkLoadRecoveryFeature` (`withNavigationErrorHandler` handing every navigation error to `ChunkLoadRecovery`, below) and `withInMemoryScrolling` (anchor scrolling for `routerLink="/o-nas" fragment="o-nas"` links; the sections carry Czech ids without diacritics: `o-nas` `o-nas`, `kontakt` `aktuality`, `/o-nas`, `kontakt`), `provideHttpClient(withXhr(), withInterceptors([authInterceptor]))` (`appHttpClient`; XMLHttpRequest because the fetch backend reports no upload progress, which the backup upload of the first setup shows), session restore on start.
- `core/reload/` and `core/navigation/`: `reload-guard.ts` (`ReloadGuard.claim(reason)`, `ReloadReason` `'chunk-load'`, `'update'`, `'unrecoverable'`): one automatic reload per reason and 60 s (`RELOAD_WINDOW_MS`), the time kept in `sessionStorage` under `nfnn.reload.<reason>` (reached through the `SESSION_STORAGE` token); when the storage cannot be read, written or read back it allows none, so a tab can never reload in a loop. `reload-place.ts` (`ReloadPlace`, 2026-11-12): right before a reload the app starts by itself, `keep(url)` writes one entry `nfnn.reload-place` to `sessionStorage`: the address without its section (a link secret travels in the fragment and is never kept), how far the page was read (`scrollY`, only when the reload stays at the address shown) and the page each tab showed (`TabMemory`, through `followTabs`); the started app takes it once and deletes it, ignores it when it is older than 60 s (`RELOAD_PLACE_LONGEST_MS`) or not of its shape, takes a tab's page only when it lies under that tab, and puts the page back where it was read once the router has scrolled the first page to its top (its `Scroll` event) and the page is tall enough (a `ResizeObserver`), at most 5 s of a visible page (`PLACE_WAIT_LONGEST_MS`) and never after the visitor's own `pointerdown`, `keydown`, `wheel` or `touchstart`. Angular's in-memory scrolling turns the browser's own restoration off (`history.scrollRestoration = 'manual'`), so without it a reload would start at the top. A kept page of a tab cannot outlive the reload, so it opens anew at the tab's page. `chunk-load-recovery.ts`: `isChunkLoadError(error)` recognises a failed dynamic import by the browsers' messages (Chromium and Firefox "dynamically imported module", Safari "Importing a module script failed", the MIME-type refusals); `ChunkLoadRecovery.handleNavigationError` sets `failure` (`{ url, initial }`, `initial` while the router has shown no page yet, `Router.navigated`), kept until the next successful navigation, and loads the page that was asked for in the newest build (`AppUpdates.loadNewVersion` of the navigation's URL, which asks the service worker for the new build first) only when the browser is online (`Connectivity.browserSaysOffline()`, `navigator.onLine`), `ChunkProbe` says the reload would land and the guard allows it; `failure` is set before that load, so a browser that stays on the page (a cancelled unload) still shows the offer; `reload()` is the visitor's own reload of it. `chunk-probe.ts`: `ChunkProbe.reloadWouldLand(error)` asks the server with `fetch(…, { method: 'HEAD', cache: 'no-store', signal })` for the chunk the error names (a same-origin `.js`/`.mjs` address in the message), aborted after 4 s (`PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS`), and answers yes, when nothing redirected the request, for a 404 or an HTML page at the chunk's own address (a deploy), or, when the message names none (Safari), for an HTML answer of `/`; another answer, a redirect, a rejected or aborted request (the network is gone or hangs while `navigator.onLine` still says online) is no, so the recovery only offers the reload. `handleNavigationError` sets `failure` before it asks, counts the router's `NavigationStart` events and ignores an answer that comes after another navigation started. It sees only router navigations: a failed `import()` outside the router (today the sign-in dialog's, #998) goes to `ChunkLoadRecovery.handleImportFailure(error)`, which reports it (`chunk-load`, or `unexpected` for another error, at `global`), sets `failure` for the page still shown (`initial` false, so the floating bar) and never reloads by itself, because the visitor asked for a dialog, not for another page (Phase 0 gate, J-m1). `ExternalNavigation.loadFromServer(url)` pushes the address as a step of the site's own history (`history.pushState`, which `own-history.ts` turns into a replace of the browser's entry) and reloads, or, for the same address or only another fragment (`/#prispet` on `/`), replaces it with `history.replaceState` and reloads, so a full load inside the site never adds a browser entry. `own-history.ts` (`installOwnHistoryIn(window)`, run by the side-effect import `install-own-history.ts` in `main.ts` before Angular starts, as the Trusted Types policy is; issue #431, Jakub 2026-11-06): the site keeps its own history on every device, so the browser holds one entry of the site and its Back (the Safari toolbar and swipe, Android's system back, the mouse's back button, Alt+←, ⌘[ on an iPad keyboard, VoiceOver's scrub) leaves the site or closes the installed app, and Safari has no page of the site to slide back under the finger. It patches `pushState`, `replaceState`, `back`, `forward` and `go` on `window.history`: a push replaces the browser's entry and is kept on the site's own stack (at most `OWN_HISTORY_LIMIT`, 50, in `sessionStorage` under `nfnn.app-history`, a name never renamed); `back()` and `go(-n)` restore the address and state of the step before with `replaceState` and then dispatch a real `PopStateEvent` on `window`, asynchronously as a browser does, so Angular's router (`navigationTrigger: 'popstate'`, `restoredState`), the scroll restoration of `withInMemoryScrolling`, `DialogHistory`, `BackTarget` and the public shell's `returningInHistory` work unchanged; `forward()` and `go(+n)` do nothing, as a native app has no forward, and `go(0)` reloads. While no one is signed in (`AuthStore.status()` `anonymous`: after a sign-out, a lost session or a start without one), `SignedOutHistory` (`signed-out-history.ts`, started with `navigationAtStart`) drops every kept step under `/sprava` or `/darce` but the current one, at once and after every navigation (`forgetOwnHistory(view, forgotten)`), so after a sign-out neither the site's Back nor the kept stack holds a donor's id, a search or a filter of the administration (security audit 2026-11-14, FRONTEND-4); a step back that is still running is left alone and the next navigation drops them. A reload on the address the kept stack ends at (an update, a receipt restart) keeps the stack; another address starts afresh; storage that cannot be read or written leaves the stack in memory, and if the layer were missing the site would simply use the browser's history as before. Safari's limit of 100 `pushState`/`replaceState` calls per 10 s is unchanged, as each push becomes one replace. The site's own steps back come only from its controls: the header's Zpět, a dialog's × and, in the app opened from the home screen of an iPhone or iPad, the swipe of `edge-swipe-back.ts`. Why: `docs/system-overview.md`, Frontend.
- `core/connectivity/` (plan v3 2.7): `Connectivity` (root, started by `connectivityAtStart` in `app.config.ts`) decides from what the API answers whether the app is offline; the one exception is the browser saying it is offline (`navigator.onLine` false at the start or its `offline` event), which is trusted at once, because a phone in airplane mode leaves requests hanging instead of failing them (Jakub 2026-11-06), and the probe that starts with it brings the app back online if the server answers; `navigator.onLine` true says nothing. `connectivityInterceptor` (the last interceptor) reports every `/api` outcome: an answer of the server (any status, or a gateway status with an `application/problem+json` body) counts as reached, while status 0 and 502, 503, 504 or 520–530 without a problem body (`isUnreachable`) count as failed; after a failure it probes at once and goes offline after 3 s (`OFFLINE_AFTER_MS`) without a success, then probes every 30 s (`PROBE_EVERY_MS`) while the tab is visible, at the window's `online` event and when the tab becomes visible again; the window's `offline` event and an answer the service worker served from its cache (`mayComeFromServiceWorker`, with a `Date` more than 15 s older than the server's clock, which it learns from network answers) only start a probe; a fresher one counts as contact while the app is online, but once it is offline it too only starts a probe, since a copy kept seconds ago reached no server. The probe (`connectivity-probe-api.ts`) is `GET /api/operations/setup` without the token, so it bypasses the service worker, with a 5 s timeout (`PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS`), after which the app is offline at once (cause `network`), without the 3 s wait a failed request starts; a probe's 5 s or a failure's 3 s that run out more than 1 s late (`SLEEP_TOLERANCE_MS`, measured with `Date.now()`) mean the device slept meanwhile (a locked iPhone or the app in the background freezes its timers, which then fire at once on waking), so the app asks the server once more instead of going offline (Jakub 2026-11-12: the label showed "Offline" after the phone woke up while the server answered in milliseconds); `wentOffline` emits each time the app goes offline. `cause` is `network` for status 0, a 504 of the worker or a browser that says offline, else `server`; `tryAgain()` probes on request. `kept-answer-refresh.ts` (`keptAnswerRefreshInterceptor`, before `serviceWorkerBypassInterceptor`): for the home content, the news and project lists and pages: while the app is offline and no service worker controls the page (so nothing can answer), an answer that does not come within 1.5 s (`OFFLINE_ANSWER_WAIT_MS`) fails as offline (status 0); with a worker in control the wait is the worker's own, which answers from its copy or gives up within 5 s, so a slow start of the worker on an old phone fails nothing it kept, so the page says it could not load and offers to try again instead of waiting; and only a request that asks for it (`REFRESH_AFTER_KEPT_COPY` through `refreshedAfterKeptCopy()`: `HomeApi.load`, both `publishedBySlug` and `NewsApi.publishedWithLatest` of the Aktuality list), answered with a copy whose `Date` is more than 15 s older than the server's clock while the app is not offline, gets the copy at once and the same request once more past the worker (`ngsw-bypass`), whose answer reaches the page as a second value only when its body differs and which gives up after 5 s (`REFRESH_WAIT_MS`); a failed, refused or unanswered second request leaves the copy and is no error, and a request sent with `ngsw-bypass` counts as a network answer (`mayComeFromServiceWorker`). Every other read gets one value and ends, because a page that gathers pages with `expand`, `reduce` or `forkJoin` (Pomáháme's `PublishedStoriesStore`) waits for each request to end, and an iPhone in airplane mode left the second request hanging, so Pomáháme said "Načítáme příběhy…" for ever (Jakub 2026-11-13); the payment details give up after 5 s (`PAYMENT_DETAILS_WAIT_MS`) as unreachable, so Přispět shows the details kept on the device instead of waiting; `e2e/pwa/offline-pages.spec.ts` opens every public page once and then again with the network hanging and with it gone. `shown-answers.ts` (`ShownAnswers`) keeps the `Date` of each public answer the current page shows (reset at a navigation to another path), whose oldest is the time in the label; `data-time.ts` writes it as `9:30` today, `4. 11. 9:30` yesterday, else a day (`15. 3. 2026`), without leading zeros except the minutes (Jakub 2026-11-06). `unseen-photos.ts` (`UnseenPhotos`, started with it) marks an `<img>` that fails to load `is-unavailable` (the global style makes it transparent, so its box and its text stay and no broken-image sign shows) and takes the mark off when it loads; offline that is a photo the service worker never kept, since it keeps only photos already seen, in the size the screen asked for, up to 300 (Jakub 2026-11-05, Q8). `browserSaysOffline()` replaces the former `core/navigation/connectivity.ts` for the error reporter and the chunk-load recovery.
- `core/device/crypto/` (plan v3, 2.13; called by the store of 2.14, and by the administration copy from 2.19): `copy-keys.ts` (`OpenCopy.create()`, `sealRecord`/`openRecord` at a `RecordAddress`, `wrapWithPassword`/`wrapWithPrf`/`wrapWithSessionKey`, `rekeyed()`, which closes the old copy at once, so a caller reads every record first, re-keys, then seals and writes them all again, outside one IndexedDB transaction, which would commit across the WebCrypto awaits; `close()`; `openWithPassword`, `openWithPrf`, `openWithSessionKey`, `generateSessionKey`; refusals as `CopyRefused` `wrong-secret`, `wrong-wrap`, `damaged`, `closed`), `password-key.ts` (`deriveWithMeasuredParameters`: Argon2id 128 MiB when the worker timed it within 1 s, else 64 MiB; `WorkerPasswordKeyDerivation` starts `argon2.worker.ts` per derivation, 30 s timeout; the messages in `password-key-protocol.ts`, which the worker imports), `argon2.ts` (hash-wasm, only for the worker and tests), `prf.ts` (`prfInput` from the device salt; `readPrf` over the device's passkeys or any of the site, giving the passkey, its user handle and the output, which the caller zeroes), `device-registry.ts` (`lookupTag`, `userHandleTag`, `registryEntry`, no e-mail in the clear), `hkdf.ts`, `device-bytes.ts` (`framed` length-prefixed fields for AAD and HKDF info). How it is secured: `docs/security-overview.md`, "Device copies".
- `core/version/` (plan v3 2.3): `api-generation.ts` (`API_GENERATION`, the server API generation the build speaks, 3), kept equal to `api-generation.json` by its spec, which the backend's `ApiGenerationTest` keeps equal to `VersionHeadersFilter.API_GENERATION`; the same files hold `POSTS_ACROSS_GENERATIONS` (the session refresh, the sign-out and the receipt status question), which both the interceptor and the filter let through, kept equal the same way; `server-version.ts` (`ServerVersion.observe(headers)` keeps the latest `Nfnn-Api` that is a whole number from 1 to 999 999 999 and the latest `Nfnn-Revision` the server would accept from a report (`revisionOf`, or `dev`), ignores anything else; signals `revision`, `apiGeneration`, `mustUpdate`, true while the latest generation differs from `APP_API_GENERATION`, a token that defaults to `API_GENERATION`); `version-interceptor.ts` (`versionInterceptor`, after `authInterceptor` in `app.config.ts`, so it also sees the refresh and the retried call): on every `/api/` call it sets `Nfnn-App: <APP_REVISION>` and `Nfnn-App-Api: <APP_API_GENERATION>` and reads the answer's headers, a refusal's too (not a status 0), which `ServerVersion` takes only from an answer whose `Cache-Control` says `no-store` (a cached answer may carry an old server's headers); while `mustUpdate`, a `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH` or `DELETE` other than `/api/donors/auth/refresh`, `/api/donors/auth/logout` and `/api/donations/receipts/status` is not sent and fails with an `HttpErrorResponse` 426 whose body is the problem `https://nfnn.cz/problems/app/update-required` (`UPDATE_REQUIRED`), title "Web mezitím dostal novou verzi", detail "Nic jsme neodeslali. Načtěte stránku znovu a zkuste to ještě jednou." (`layout.update.problem.*`). Why: `docs/system-overview.md`, Frontend.
- `core/security/trusted-types.ts` (plan v3, 2.5): `installScriptUrlPolicy(trustedTypes, document.baseURI)`, run by `install-trusted-types.ts`, the first import of `main.ts` (so before any other module of the app is evaluated), creates the Trusted Types policy `default` with only `createScriptURL`: `admittedScriptUrl(input, sink, base)` gives the worker constructor (`WORKER_SINK`) an address of the site's own origin, without user name, query or fragment, whose path is `/worker-<8 characters>.js` (the receipt PDF and QR reader workers as the build names them), the service worker registration (`SERVICE_WORKER_SINK`) only `/ngsw-worker.js`, each resolved, and `null` (refused) for any other address or sink; without `trustedTypes`, with a default policy already there or when the browser refuses the policy, nothing is installed and the app starts anyway. The Content-Security-Policy that requires it is in `nginx-security-headers.conf` (`docs/security-overview.md`, Browser and transport).
- `core/live/` (plan v3 2.16): `live-api.ts` asks `GET /api/live` as a text stream (`observe: 'events'`, `reportProgress`), `event-stream.ts` (`EventStreamReader`) cuts the growing text into events (`event:` and `data:` lines, comments skipped), and `live-updates.ts` (`LiveUpdates`, root) keeps one stream while a donor is signed in, a page listens (`changes(feeds)`, which emits on a hint of one of those feeds and once after a reopening that follows a stream lost while it listened, since hints in between are lost; a page that started listening while the stream waited for a retry is told too, since hints of the wait are lost to it, while a page whose arrival opened the stream again has just read everything and is not told) and the tab was not hidden for 30 s; `connected` says whether the revision event of the open stream has arrived. A stream that streamed for 10 s and ended opens again within a second, a failure after 1 s doubling up to 60 s with jitter, a 401 or 403 (after the interceptor's one refresh) not before the session is renewed (a new sign-in or the next token refresh). A stream that streamed for 10 s and then failed starts its retries again from 1 s; each attempt of the request (`Sent`, such as the interceptor's retry after a refresh) is read from its own start. `authInterceptor` reads a problem from a text body too (`application/problem+json` as text), so the stream's 401 `auth/expired-token` refreshes like any call. Specs get `testing/live-fixtures.ts` (`FakeLiveUpdates`: `hint(feed)`, `resync()`, `connected`, `listeners()`) from `src/test-providers.ts`, so no spec opens a stream unless it asks for the real service.
- `core/updates/` and the service worker (plan v3 2.4): `angular-service-worker.ts` (`provideAppServiceWorker()`, in `app.config.ts`) registers Angular's `ngsw-worker.js` (`SERVICE_WORKER_SCRIPT`, a relative name, which the Trusted Types policy resolves against the base URL) with `registerWhenStable:30000`, off in development, and hands `SwUpdate` to the rest of the app as `SERVICE_WORKER_UPDATES` (`service-worker-updates.ts`, `NO_SERVICE_WORKER` without one, so specs and a browser without service workers take the same path). `app-updates.ts` (`AppUpdates.start()` from an app initializer) loads a new version by itself, with no button and no bar (Jakub 2026-11-12: "Všude automaticky", on the web and in the installed apps alike; the iOS UX designer and the iOS/PWA developer of that decision chose the moments so the reload is not seen mid-reading). Only at a free moment: no half-typed form (`DirtyForms.any()`), no running work (`RunningWork.any()`), no open `dialog`, no playing `video` or `audio` (the camera of `/overit`), and not offline (`Connectivity.offline()` or `navigator.onLine` false), because a reload of a page the worker never kept would fail. A ready version (`VERSION_READY`), with which the running app still works, takes over at two moments: right away within `START_UP_WINDOW_MS` (10 s) of the start, before the first `pointerdown`, `keydown`, `scroll`, `wheel` or `touchstart`, while the page is still at the address it started at (a page that changed its own address, as one that took a link code off it, counts as touched); at the next router navigation to another path (a change of only the query or the section, `?strana=2` or `#o-nas`, stays in the app), as a full load of that address (`ExternalNavigation.loadFromServer`), and when the page is not free the navigation runs in the app and the load follows at its end once it is. Never when the tab or the installed app goes into the background or comes back: a visitor who switches apps often does it for this very page (the authenticator during the second-factor setup, the bank app from the payment panel, the file picker of Android), and a reload would throw away what the page shows; the reviews of the decision found those cases, so the background moment the advisers first proposed was dropped. Not after idle time either, which looks like reading. Two states are urgent, because the running app can no longer do everything: the server speaks another API generation (`ServerVersion.mustUpdate()`, writes are held) and the worker can no longer serve the tab's version (`SwUpdate.unrecoverable`, reported as `sw-unrecoverable`/`service-worker`); then the page loads again at once, at the same address, as soon as it is free (an effect over the signals of the free moment, checked again after a `submit`, `reset`, a dialog's `close` or a video's `pause` or `ended`), and otherwise at the next navigation. For a new generation it loads only once per pair of the server's generation and the app's build in a tab (`nfnn.update.loaded-for` in `sessionStorage`; without the storage it does not load), so a server that lags behind or was rolled back never makes the page reload over and over: the writes stay held and a refused send says so at its form. With the worker it first asks it for the new build (at most `UPDATE_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`, 10 s, not when a version is already ready) and loads only when the worker found one and the page is still free, since a load would otherwise bring back the same build; a `VERSION_READY` that comes later loads it then. Every automatic load is one `ReloadGuard.claim` (`'update'`, or `'unrecoverable'`) and keeps the place first (`ReloadPlace.keep`); a refused claim leaves the page as it is until a later moment, with no bar. An answer naming another build than the app's (`ServerVersion.revision` against `APP_REVISION`) asks the worker to look for an update once per build, and so does a page the browser brings back from its memory of pages (`pageshow` with `persisted`), which never sees the `VERSION_READY` it missed. `VERSION_INSTALLATION_FAILED` reports `sw-install`/`service-worker`. `loadNewVersion(url)` keeps the place, asks the worker for the new build (the same 10 s) and then loads `url` from the server; `ChunkLoadRecovery` loads through it. Never `activateUpdate()` before a reload: the worker serves the newest ready version to the load anyway, and switching the old running tab would break its lazy chunks. `running-work.ts` (`RunningWork`: `start()` returns its end, `until(promise)`, `during(observable)` from subscription to its end, `any()` a signal) holds work a reload would cut off: every `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` under `/api/` until its answer (`running-writes.ts`, `runningWritesInterceptor`, the first interceptor of `appHttpClient`, so a refresh and a retried call count too), a passkey ceremony (`PasskeyAuthenticator`), a receipt drawing (`ReceiptPdf.draw` and `drawBundle`), a password key derivation of the device copy (`PASSWORD_KEY_DERIVATION`), the passkey that unlocks the administration's copy (`DeviceVault.unlockAdministrationWithPasskey`) and a backup download (`BackupDownloads`); an upload of a backup is a write. A share sheet is not held: only a reload for a new generation could close it, which needs the two to meet. `dirty-forms.ts` (`DirtyForms.register(check)`, `any()`, a throwing check counts as dirty) and `tracked-form.ts` (`form[appTrackedForm]`, a host directive of `appFocusFirstInvalid`, so every form of the site registers `NgForm.dirty` while the form is in the document, a form on a page kept out of view by `KeptTabPages` counting as not half-typed; the administration's settings form, which has no `appFocusFirstInvalid`, carries it itself). `boot-report.ts` (`markCodeArrived(document)`, the first statement of `main.ts`, sets `data-nfnn-loading` on `<html>`, so the watchdog knows the code arrived and the start only waits; `BootReport.started()`, called by `main.ts` once the app is bootstrapped) sets `data-nfnn-started` on `<html>` and, when `public/boot-guard.js` had to step in during this tab's start (`sessionStorage` `nfnn.boot.recovered`), reports `boot`/`start-up` once. `public/boot-guard.js` (a plain script of the build, loaded by `index.html` before the app, allowed by the policy as `'self'`): when the app has not set `data-nfnn-started` 15 s after the page began, it waits in 15 s steps while `data-nfnn-loading` is set (the code arrived and the start waits on the network, such as a session refresh during an outage) up to 60 s and then shows the panel without a reload; when the code never arrived, it reloads once per 60 s (`nfnn.reload.boot`, never while `navigator.onLine` is false) and otherwise shows the full-screen panel "Web se nenačetl" (`role="alert"`, the button "Vyčistit a načíst znovu" unregisters every service worker and deletes every cache, never `localStorage`, IndexedDB or the cookies, so the sign-in and the data kept on the device stay), which goes away by itself if the app starts after all; its Czech text has no i18n ids, since it runs before Angular; `scripts/boot-guard.test.mjs` tests it in a jsdom page. The panel also offers "Stáhnout diagnostický soubor" (the help line: download it and send it to info@nfnn.cz, it holds no personal data and goes nowhere by itself): `boot-guard.js` gathers the file when the panel shows (before "Vyčistit a načíst znovu" could clear what it counts; each browser answer waits at most 2 s) and at the press shares it on an iPhone or iPad that can share files (the share sheet; a closed sheet saves nothing, a failed share saves instead) and otherwise saves it through a hidden `<a download>` on a `blob:` address revoked after the click, with a `role="status"` line naming the saved file and, when the browser refuses to make it, a `role="alert"` line that asks to write without it (the button keeps its name), built with its own ES5 copies of the closed lists, the user agent reduction and the first path segments of the routes (`core/diagnostics/` below), and keeps each start it gave up on (reloaded or shown the panel) as `nfnn.boot.last` in `sessionStorage` (`panelShown`, `codeArrived`, `waitedSeconds`, `at`), which the next file of the tab reads as `boot.previous`; the test runs every file it saves through the app's `sanitizedDiagnostics` (equal, so nothing is dropped), compares its platform with `detectPlatform` over a user agent corpus and plants canaries (`scripts/ts-import.mjs` lets Node import the schema's TypeScript). `ngsw-config.json`: the shell (`index.html`, `boot-guard.js`, the manifest, the tab icon, `icon-512.png` of the launch screen, the logos, every `main-`, `polyfills-`, `chunk-` and `styles-` file and the fonts under `/media/`) is prefetched, the workers, receipt fonts and other images are cached the first time they are asked for, every app route is a navigation served from `index.html` (never `/api/`, `/mcp`, `/.well-known/`, `/actuator/` or a file), and only public answers that are the same for everyone are kept (`public-content`, performance with `refreshAhead` 0: the home content, news and projects, served from the kept copy at once and fetched again behind it into the copy, 5 s for one never kept, 100 for 30 days; `payment-details`, freshness with 1 s, a day; `bank-logos`, performance: `/api/donations/bank-logos/images/*`, the bank logos, 60 at most for a year, listed before `bank-logo-list`, freshness with 1 s, a day, for `/api/donations/bank-logos` (the worker matches a group's pattern anywhere in the address and takes the first group that matches, so the images' group must come first); `public-photos`, performance: `/api/content/media/*`, the photos the visitor has already seen, 300 at most, Q8, given up on after 10 s when never kept and the network hangs, as the bank logos are, so an `<img>` ends as an empty box instead of loading for ever); `backend/.../ServiceWorkerCachedAnswersTest` proves each cached path answers alike with no token, a donor's and an administrator's and sets no cookie, and that the data group URLs find no other GET handler, and `scripts/ngsw-config.test.mjs` which built files land in which group. `service-worker-bypass.ts` (`serviceWorkerBypassInterceptor`, the innermost interceptor of `appHttpClient`) adds `ngsw-bypass: true` to every `/api` request that is not a `GET` of an anchored public answer (`mayComeFromServiceWorker`), because Angular matches a data group URL anywhere in the address; `versionInterceptor` ignores the version headers of an answer that may be a copy the worker kept (`mayComeFromServiceWorker` while `SERVICE_WORKER_UPDATES.isEnabled`), so a weeks-old copy never names an old generation. `core/install/app-install.ts` (`AppInstall`): `way()` is `prompt` while the browser offered `beforeinstallprompt` (kept and prevented), `ios-share` on an iPhone or iPad, `mac-dock` in Safari on a Mac, `none` once installed or when the page runs as the app (`(display-mode: standalone)` or `navigator.standalone`); `offered()` is false for 90 days after "Teď ne" (`localStorage` `nfnn.install-offer.hidden-until`). `core/auth/app-entry-guard.ts`: `/aplikace`, the manifest's `start_url` and `id`, sends a signed-in administrator to `/sprava`, a signed-in donor to `/darce/prehled` and anyone else to `/` without opening the sign-in dialog (#349: a modal at launch focused the e-mail field and raised the keyboard; the header's "Přihlásit se" offers the sign-in, and the experts of #349 kept the role-based start after the app was closed instead of reopening the last screen), after one session refresh when the `nfnn.donor-session` flag says a session may exist. The built `ngsw-worker.js` begins with the site's payment route (`frontend/service-worker/payment-route.js`, prepended by `scripts/wrap-service-worker.mjs`, the `postbuild` of `npm run build`, once, which fails when a build with `ngsw.json` keeps a worker without it; the registered address and the Trusted Types policy stay the same, and the stack probe checks that the image's worker begins with it): a `message` and a `fetch` listener registered before Angular's, which take only their own message and the same-origin path `/platba.spayd` and stop the event there, never the network and nothing under `/api/`. The message `{ type: 'nfnn-payment-file', payment }` with a SPAYD text (`SPD*1.0*` and the QR alphanumeric characters `spayd()` writes, at most 1 000 characters) reserves it in the worker's memory for 10 s under a random id (at most 16 kept, the oldest giving way), answered on the message's port (`id: null` for anything else); `GET /platba.spayd?id=<id>`, the id as its only parameter, serves that text once as `platba.spayd` (`Content-Disposition: inline`, `no-store`, `nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox allow-downloads; frame-ancestors 'none'`), any other address 404 (a navigation 204, so a tab whose worker restarted between the reservation and the tap stays on the payment panel with its notice) and any other method 405, both as sandboxed plain text; so the address never carries a payment, and a link from elsewhere cannot make the site serve one. `scripts/service-worker-payment-route.test.mjs` runs the route in a VM and the wrap, and `e2e/pwa/payment-route.spec.ts` proves it behind the real nginx.
- `core/push/` (slice 2.28): push goes through Angular's own `ngsw-worker.js` and `SwPush`, never a worker of the site's own (Trusted Types admit only `/ngsw-worker.js`), into which the build prepends only the payment route and the badge (`service-worker/app-badge.js`, below). `service-worker-push.ts` holds the token `SERVICE_WORKER_PUSH` (`NO_PUSH` by default, so development, specs and a browser without a worker take one path) with `isEnabled`, `controllerChanges`, `messages`, `notificationClicks` (`{action, data}`), `requestPermission()` (asks `Notification.requestPermission()` at once, so it runs inside the click's gesture, and answers `granted`, `denied` or `default`), `requestSubscription(publicKey)` → `{endpoint, p256dh, auth}` (`devicePushSubscriptionOf`, from `PushSubscription.toJSON().keys`, refused without them; a subscription the browser still holds under another `applicationServerKey`, as after the server replaced its push key, is ended first, because the browser refuses to subscribe again over it), `browserSubscription()` (`subscribed`, `not-subscribed` or `unknown`) and `unsubscribe()` (a device that was not subscribed counts as done). The last two and the check before subscribing read `navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistration()` → `pushManager.getSubscription()` themselves instead of `SwPush`, whose calls wait for a worker that controls the page and so never settle after a hard reload or with the server's switch off; each gives up after `PUSH_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 s), so a button that waits on one always settles; `core/updates/angular-service-worker.ts`, the only file that imports `@angular/service-worker`, provides it from `SwPush`, enabled only while a worker controls the page and the browser has `PushManager` and `Notification`. ngsw itself shows the notification of a message (`notification` of the payload) and on a click focuses the open app or opens the payload's `onActionClick` address. `alert-routes.ts` is the app's own closed table of the kinds (`test` → `/sprava/upozorneni`, `bank-import-*` → `/sprava`, `backups-*` → `/sprava/zalohy`, `mail-*` → `/sprava/nastaveni`, the donor's `donor-test` → `/darce/udaje` `gift-arrived` → `/darce/dary` and `yearly-receipt-ready` → `/darce/potvrzeni`, anything else → `/sprava/upozorneni`) and reads a message's `count` (a whole number 0–999, `MAX_OPEN_ALERT_COUNT`, the backend's `PushMessage.MAX_COUNT`, else nothing; a donor's message carries none, so neither the app nor `service-worker/app-badge.js` changes the badge for it); its kinds are the shared cases `shared-cases/push/notification.json` (kind → tag and route), which `alert-routes.spec.ts` runs and `e2e/push-device.ts` reads its messages from. `alert-notification-clicks.ts` (`AlertNotificationClicks.start()`) routes a click the open app hears with `Router.navigateByUrl` by the kind alone, never to an address taken from the message, so the unsaved-changes guard still asks, and only in the tab that is visible and has the focus (ngsw focuses one tab and then tells every tab, so the others stay where they are); `app-badge.ts` (`AppBadge.start()`, `show(count)`) sets `navigator.setAppBadge(count)` or `clearAppBadge()` for each message with a count (the test alert carries the open count too) and when the alerts page loads, feature-detected and never throwing, and clears the badge whenever the app knows its session may see no alert: when the restore at start finds no session, or one without the role (a sign-out, erasure or role removal while the app was closed, #1000), when the signed-in administrator signs out or loses the role, and again each time the app comes back to the foreground in that state; it leaves the badge while the session is still being restored and while the visitor only waits signed out because the server gave no answer or the app is offline (`AuthStore.waitsSignedOut`, the session hint kept), so an outage of the server or the network never wipes the count of an administrator's open alerts. A closed app's badge is set by the worker itself (Jakub 2026-11-12, after the icon kept a "1" of a fault long resolved): `frontend/service-worker/app-badge.js`, prepended after the payment route by `npm run build` (`scripts/wrap-service-worker.mjs`, `WORKER_PREFIX`), listens to `push` and, when the payload's `count` is a whole number 0–999, calls `self.navigator.setAppBadge(count)` or `clearAppBadge()` inside `waitUntil`, feature-detected, a refusal swallowed; it reads nothing else, stores nothing, asks no network and leaves the notification to ngsw. `open-alerts.ts` (`OpenAlerts.start()`, root, from the same initializer as `AppBadge`) holds the open alerts the server names (`alerts`, `null` until read and for anyone who is not an administrator) through `open-alerts-api.ts` (`OpenAlertsApi.openAlerts()`, `GET /api/admin/operations/push`, its `openAlerts`): it reads them for a signed-in administrator on any page, not only in the administration, at the start of the session, when the app comes back to the foreground at most once a minute (`OPEN_ALERTS_REFRESH_GAP_MS`) and at once for every push message with a count the open app hears, and each answer sets the badge to its count, so a fault resolved while the app was closed clears the badge at the next opening; a failure keeps the badge, and an answer that arrives after the administrator signed out is dropped. Both start from an app initializer in `app.config.ts`. `push-availability.ts` tells the alerts page why a device cannot subscribe.
- `core/errors/`: `error-vocabulary.ts` holds the closed lists a browser error report may carry (`ERROR_KINDS` `unexpected`, `chunk-load`, `pdf-render`, `pdf-load`, `qr-encode`, `verify-parse`, `verify-signature`, `boot`, `sw-install`, `sw-unrecoverable`; `ERROR_PLACES` `global`, `navigation`, `start-up`, `service-worker`; `ERROR_NAMES`, the standard error and `DOMException` names plus `HttpErrorResponse` and `Other`; `BROWSERS`, `OPERATING_SYSTEMS`, `DISPLAY_MODES`), and `npm run errors:vocabulary` (`scripts/export-error-vocabulary.mjs`) writes them to `error-vocabulary.json`, which the spec keeps equal to the TypeScript and the backend's `ClientErrorVocabularyTest` to its enums. `client-platform.ts` (`detectPlatform`) turns the user agent into a browser family, its major version (1–999, else none) and an OS family, nothing more; `app-revision.ts` reads `NFNN_APP_REVISION`, which the image's build defines (`--define`), and keeps it only when it is what the server accepts (`revisionOf`: 7–40 lowercase hex digits or `dev`), `dev` otherwise, so a build with an odd revision never makes every report a 400. `error-reporter.ts` (`ErrorReporter.report(kind, place, error?)`) never throws: it keeps the error's `name` only when it is in `ERROR_NAMES` (`Other` otherwise) and never reads its message or stack, sends one report per kind, place and name every 10 minutes and counts the repeats it held back into the next one's `count` (at most 100), sends at most 10 per page load, keeps up to 20 while `Connectivity.browserSaysOffline()` says offline and sends them on the window's `online` event, and posts with a raw `fetch` (`keepalive`, `credentials: 'omit'`, no interceptor, a failure ignored) to `/api/operations/client-errors`. `reporting-error-handler.ts`: `ReportingErrorHandler` extends Angular's `ErrorHandler`, keeps its console output and reports `unexpected`/`global`, except a failed chunk (`isChunkLoadError`), which `ChunkLoadRecovery` reports itself, so a lost chunk is one report per page load; `app.config.ts` provides it as `appErrorHandler` (`app.config.spec.ts` checks `appConfig.providers` holds it). `ChunkLoadRecovery.handleNavigationError` reports `chunk-load`/`navigation` for a failed chunk. The receipt PDF (`ReceiptPdf`: `pdf-load`, `pdf-render`) and the verification page (`ReceiptVerifier`: `verify-signature`; `QrReader`: `qr-encode`) report their failures with place `global` (below); their specs assert the exact closed body through `core/errors/testing/client-error-reports.ts`. Specs: `error-reporter.spec.ts` (the canary `Error('Zxqv Kanárek 7781,77 Kč 2099-778177')` arrives as closed values only, dedupe, the per-load limit, the offline queue, the allowlist), `client-platform.spec.ts`, `error-vocabulary.spec.ts`, `reporting-error-handler.spec.ts`; `app-revision.spec.ts`; Playwright `e2e/client-errors.spec.ts` (an error thrown on the privacy page posts one report of closed values; a lost chunk posts exactly one `chunk-load` report per page load and no `unexpected` one; a receipt font gone after a deploy posts one `pdf-load` report named `NotFoundError`, and the page loads once more and finishes the download).
- `core/files/file-download.ts` `saveBlob(file)` (a `SavedFile`, `{ blob, fileName }`; the receipt PDF, the payment file, backups, the backup key and the diagnostic file save through it): object URL on a hidden `<a download>` clicked inside the page, revoked in `setTimeout(…, 0)` (Safari and Firefox cancel a synchronously revoked download).
- `core/diagnostics/` (the diagnostic file, Jakub 2026-11-06; how it is secured: `docs/security-overview.md`, "Diagnostic file (in the browser)"): `diagnostics-schema.ts` (no Angular, so `scripts/read-diagnostics.mjs` and the boot watchdog's test import it too) holds the file's schema, `DiagnosticsFile`, and `sanitizedDiagnostics(raw)`, which builds the file from its fixed paths only, never copying or spreading its input, and turns every leaf outside its closed list, pattern or range into `null` (an event outside the lists is dropped, a `detail` is kept only for its own kind, at most the 50 latest events), with `pageOf(path)` (the route pattern of `DIAGNOSTIC_PAGES`, kept equal to the nginx routes by its spec, else `other`), `ageBucket`, `createdAtOf` and `diagnosticsFileName` (`nfnn-diagnostika-<YYYY-MM-DD>-<HHMM>.json`, UTC). `diagnostic-events.ts` (`DiagnosticEvents`, root) keeps the events of this page in memory only, at most 50 of the last 24 h: `ErrorReporter.report(kind, place, error?, detail?)` records every report, also those its repeat window or per-load cap holds back, with its closed `detail` (the receipt PDF's failure reason), which is never sent, and `DonorCopySync` records each sync's outcome as `copy-sync`; `AuthStore` empties it when a signed-in donor's session ends and when another account signs in. `device-copy-diagnostics.ts`: the token `DEVICE_COPY_DIAGNOSTICS` (`null` by default; `app.config.ts` asks `DeviceCopies.diagnostics()`, which answers `null` until a session loaded the vault and then `DeviceVault.diagnostics()`: mode, donor copy `none`/`open`/`locked`, age bucket of the stored copy, administration copy `none`/`locked`/`open`). `diagnostics.ts` (`Diagnostics.file(source)` and `download(source)`) gathers the closed state (the revision, the start attributes, `platformOf` of `client-platform.ts`, the page, `Connectivity`, feature support, `persisted` and `nearlyFull` from the storage estimate, service worker and cache counts, the ages of `nfnn.reload.*` and `nfnn.boot.last`, the session's kind and account `none`/`donor`/`administrator`, only `signed-in` and no administration state in a `PUBLIC` session, the device copy and the events), each browser answer waiting at most 2 s, and `prepare` returns the JSON as a blob with its name, `save` saves it through `saveBlob`, and `needsSecondTap` and `share` hand it to the share sheet on an iPhone or iPad (`DIAGNOSTICS_NAVIGATOR`). ESLint refuses in this folder reading a donor, a token, an e-mail, a name, an open copy or its contents, the address beyond its path, the title, a cookie, the referrer, the client hints, `localStorage`, an error's message or stack (as a member, by brackets or by destructuring), any `setItem`, `removeItem`, `clear()` or assignment on `sessionStorage` and spreading an object (`scripts/diagnostics-confinement.test.mjs`). Specs: `diagnostics-schema.spec.ts` (exact keys, forged values, `__proto__`, the routes), `diagnostic-events.spec.ts`, `diagnostics.spec.ts` (canaries in the path, query and fragment, title, cookie, both storages, an error and an HTTP error, the donor's name and e-mail, the user agent, a cache name, a service worker scope and script, the referrer, `/overit#…` and `/pripojeni?…`, found by `raw-scan.ts`'s `textDump` and `leaks` in plain, URL-encoded, base64 and hex form; the client hints never read; nothing written in a `PUBLIC` session).
- `app.routes.ts`: `''` → `layout/public-shell` (header, tab bar, no footer) with children `'obnova-hesla'` → `features/donors/password-reset/` (title "Nové heslo | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'potvrzeni-emailu'` → `features/donors/email-verification/` (title "Potvrzení e-mailu | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'pripojeni'` → `features/donors/connect-app/` (title "Připojení aplikace | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'prvni-nastaveni'` → `features/operations/setup/first-setup` (title "První nastavení | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'overit'` → `features/donations/verification/verification-page` (title "Ověření potvrzení o daru | Nová naděje – nadační fond") and `''` → `features/content/content.routes.ts` (`''` home, `'zasady-ochrany-osobnich-udaju'` privacy policy, `'aktuality'` → `features/content/news-page/news-page` and `'projekty'` → `features/content/projects-page/projects-page` (titles "Aktuality | Nová naděje – nadační fond", "Kde pomáháme | …"), `'aktuality/:slug'` and `'projekty/:slug'` → `features/content/detail/content-detail` with route data `kind` `news` / `project` (the tab title becomes "<title> | Nová naděje – nadační fond" once loaded), `'**'` not-found; a route cannot have both `children` and `loadChildren`, so the content routes hang under a child of their own); `'darce'` → `layout/donor-shell` with `features/donors/donors.routes.ts` behind `core/auth/donor-guard.ts`: one parent `''` → `features/donors/dashboard/donor-area` (the donor area) with the sections `'prehled'` (title "Můj přehled | Nová naděje – nadační fond"), `'dary'` ("Moje dary | …"), `'potvrzeni'` ("Potvrzení o daru | …"), `'ucty'` ("Bankovní účty | …"), `'udaje'` ("Údaje a zabezpečení | …") and `''` redirecting to `'prehled'`; `'sprava'` → the same `donor-shell` with `features/admin/admin.routes.ts` (`''` → `features/admin/home/`, title "Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'platby'` → `features/admin/payments/`, title "Platby z banky | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'spravci'` → `features/admin/administrators/`, title "Správci | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'starsi-symboly'` → `features/admin/symbol-claims/`, title "Odpovědi o starších symbolech | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'loga-bank'` → `features/admin/bank-logos/`, title "Loga bank | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'historie'` → `features/admin/actions/`, title "Historie změn | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'prehledy'` → `features/admin/report/`, title "Měsíční e-mail | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'zalohy'` → `features/admin/backups/`, title "Zálohy | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'upozorneni'` → `features/admin/alerts/`, title "Upozornění | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'logy'` → `features/admin/logs/`, title "Logy a diagnostika | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'nastaveni'` → `features/admin/settings/`, title "Nastavení | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'aktuality'` → `features/admin/news/admin-news`, title "Aktuality | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'aktuality/nova'` and `'aktuality/:id'` → `features/admin/news/admin-news-editor`, titles "Nová aktualita | Správa | …" and "Úprava aktuality | Správa | …"; `'projekty'` → `features/admin/projects/admin-projects`, title "Pomáháme | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'projekty/nova'` and `'projekty/:id'` → `features/admin/projects/admin-project-editor`, titles "Nový příběh | Správa | …" and "Úprava příběhu | Správa | …"; `'darci'` → `features/admin/donors/`, title "Dárci | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond"; `'darci/:id'` → `features/admin/donor-detail/`, title "Dárce | Správa | Nová naděje – nadační fond") behind `core/auth/admin-guard.ts`. `'donor'` redirects to `'darce'` with the rest of the path (the earlier English address). Every address a visitor sees is Czech without diacritics; only `/api/...` stays English. Unknown addresses keep their URL and show `not-found`. nginx answers exactly these addresses with the app and status 200 (the map `$app_page` of `frontend/nginx.conf`, one line per route, a parameter as `[^/]+`, no trailing slash, because the router shows `not-found` for `/o-nas/` too) and every other path that is no file with the same `index.html` and status 404 (issue #350, Jakub 2026-11-06: nothing changes for the visitor, only the status is right); `app.routes.spec.ts` walks these routes (lazy children loaded, a prefix redirect such as `'donor'` taking the target's addresses) and fails unless the list equals the map, read by `scripts/app-routes.mjs` through the global setup `src/testing/server-routes-setup.ts`, so a new route adds its line there in the same change.
- `core/device/status/` (plan v3, 2.11): `device-status-api.ts` (`POST /api/donors/devices/status` with `WITHOUT_ACCESS_TOKEN`, listed in `eslint.config.js`) and `device-status-source.ts` (`DEVICE_STATUS_SOURCE`: the answer within 10 s, checked against the closed list by `statusAnswer`, else `null`, so a failure never deletes) and `device-status-retry.ts` (2.21: `DeviceStatusRetry`, started with `Connectivity` in `app.config.ts`, calls `SESSION_DEVICE.connectionReturned()` each time the app goes from offline to online; `DeviceCopies` passes it on only to a vault already loaded, and the vault asks again only when its last question went unanswered). How it is secured: `docs/security-overview.md`, "Device status".
- `core/device/store/` (plan v3, 2.14; how it is secured: `docs/security-overview.md`, "Device copies"): `device-storage.ts` (the `DeviceStorage` port: device salt, registry entries, session keys and sealed records, every `write(change)` atomic; the database `nfnn.device`), `idb-device-storage.ts` (the only importer of idb, `eslint-confinement.js`; five object stores since 2.20e (database version 2), one `readwrite` transaction per change, each read in its own `readonly` transaction awaiting both the request and `tx.done` (idb's read shortcuts leave a failed transaction's `done` unobserved), a read that fails with `UnknownError` or `InvalidStateError` (WebKit drops the connection of a suspended home-screen app without a `close` event, #894) closes the connection and opens it again once, opening given 5 s, `blocking` closes the connection so another tab can delete the database, `destroy()` deletes it; `idbDeviceStoragePresent()` asks `indexedDB.databases()` so a sign-out never makes an empty database), `memory-device-storage.ts` (the same in memory, for memory-only mode), `account-store.ts` (`AccountStore` of one open copy and feed: `load()` → `empty`, `loaded` or `damaged`, `replace(contents)` seals one record per chunk and one for the summary under random record ids, the signal `contents`; from 2.20e `drafts()`, `keepDraft(draft)` and `dropDraft(key)`: the unsaved forms in the object store `drafts`, sealed as drafts, which `replace` never touches), `device-vault.ts` (`DeviceVault`, root; from 2.19 also the administration's copy: `administration` (the open copy, `reason` `sign-in`, `empty` or `unlocked`), `stores`, `administrationPasskeys`, `unlockAdministrationWithPasskey` (the PRF ceremony through `PASSKEY_READER`, outside the vault lock, and nothing opens when a sign-in or sign-out came in between), `unlockAdministrationWithPassword`, `lockAdministration`, `administrationRevoked` (deletes the administration copy and keeps the donor's), closed whenever `AdministrationAccess` locks; `drafts`, `keepDraft`, `dropDraft` (2.20e, under the vault lock, nothing for a store kept only in memory or closed), `signedIn`, `restored`, `signedOut`, `signedOutElsewhere`, `sessionLost`, `forget`, `passwordChanged`, `passwordRemoved`, `passkeyAdded`, `prfInput`, `replaceContents`, `withCopyLock`; `signedIn` and `restored` take `storeNothing`, which `AuthStore` sets for a `PUBLIC` session (2.10), so a "Cizí počítač" copy lives only in memory, also after a reload; signals `opened` (`{ store, reason: 'sign-in' | 'restored' | 'empty', account }`), `mode` (`stored` | `memory`) and `locked` (a copy of the account is on the device but this session cannot open it); tokens `DEVICE_STORAGE_OPENER`, `DEVICE_STORAGE_PRESENT`, `PASSWORD_KEY_DERIVATION`, `COPY_CHANNELS`, `STORAGE_PERSISTENCE`; Web Locks `nfnn.device-vault` and `nfnn.device-copy-write.<id>`, `BroadcastChannel` `nfnn.device-copy.<id>`, `localStorage` flag `nfnn.device-copy`), the device status of 2.11 (each registry entry keeps the `deviceSecret` of the sign-in that opened it, replaced at every sign-in and dropped by one without it; an administrator's sign-in writes the same secret into the administration copy, a locked one included, which keeps it when unlocked; `signedOut` and `sessionLost` leave the session wraps for later when the storage still fails after that reopen, and the vault tries once more at each later occasion: its next call (before the call's own work, under its lock), the page becoming visible or shown again (`visibilitychange`, `pageshow`), and the next start, whose `restore()` runs `sessionLost()` first because the ended session cleared the session hint; `signedIn` and `restored` drop the pending removal; after `signedOut`, `sessionLost` and `restored` the vault asks `DEVICE_STATUS_SOURCE` once per secret held, outside its lock, and deletes on `wipe-all` or `unknown` every copy of that account, on `wipe-admin` or `administrator: false` its administration copies only, then announces `deleted` and, for a donor copy, shows the signed-out notice "Data z tohoto zařízení jsme smazali a odhlásili jsme vás."; nothing is asked while a "Cizí počítač" copy is open), `testing/raw-scan.ts` (every stored value as text, its bytes and its base64, hex and URL decodings, for the canaries) and `testing/fake-device.ts` (named Web Locks and a channel bus for two tabs in one spec). `core/auth/session-device.ts` is what `AuthStore` tells the device (`SESSION_DEVICE`, a no-op by default, so specs and pages without the vault change nothing; `deviceAccountOf(donor)` with the passkey user handle of the donor's id and `administrator` for the `ADMIN` role); `core/auth/administration-access.ts` (`AdministrationAccess`, root, 2.19: locked at every start, unlocked by a sign-in in this tab and by the lock panel, locked again by sign-out and after `ADMINISTRATION_LOCK_AFTER_MS`, five minutes, with the page hidden or the device asleep (`ACCESS_CLOCK` reads both `performance.now()` and `Date.now()`, and either clock having moved five minutes, or the wall clock having gone back, locks it, because the monotonic clock stops while Apple devices, Android and Linux sleep; while the page is visible and unlocked, `ADMINISTRATION_PRESENCE_CHECK_MS`, every 15 s, compares with the previous check, so a computer that slept with the window open wakes locked, #999; checked again on `visibilitychange`, `resume` and `pageshow`), `keepsPage` set when a form is dirty as it locks); the app provides `device-copies.ts` (`DeviceCopies`), which loads the vault and `features/donations/device-copy/` with `import()` at the first session event, so neither is in the bundle that loads first, or already when the sign-in dialog opens (`prepare()`, 2.19), because a passkey sign-in waits only 1.5 s for the device's PRF input and a copy signed in without it gets no passkey wrap. `features/donations/device-copy/`: `donor-sync-api.ts` (`/api/donations/me/sync` and `/sync/chunks?ids=`, at most 64 ids), `donor-copy-sync.ts` (`DonorCopySync`, 2.15: syncs a copy whenever one opens, at sign-in, restored or empty, and on `sync()`, `reload()` (every chunk again) and `catchUp()` (nothing within 30 s of the last sync, and it joins a sync that runs); one sync at a time and at most one queued after it, which a `reload()` turns into a full one; under the copy lock it reads the stored copy, asks for the manifest and only the chunks whose digest changed, checks every digest with `canonical-rows.ts`, tries twice, gives each request 30 s (`SYNC_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, so the copy lock is never held for ever), refuses an answer whose `subject` is not the open copy's donor (unreported: the session changed, not the data), keeps the signature and portrait of every active receipt (fetched through `ReceiptsApi.image` and kept only when they give their digest) and stores the whole copy with `DeviceVault.replaceContents`; then compares the copy's own figures with its summary, downloads it whole again at most once an hour when they differ and reports `device-copy-mismatch` at most once an hour (both pauses kept in memory, so per open tab); signals `syncing`, `open`, `view`, `firstSync` and `keepsOnDevice`), `donor-copy.ts` (`DonorCopyLink`, root: the `DonorCopy` the pages read, set by `DonorCopySync` when `device-copies.ts` loads it, so the donor pages reach the copy without importing the vault), `donor-copy-view.ts` (pure: `donorCopyView(contents)` turns the stored chunks into the summary, the gifts as `DonationItem`s in the order the API lists them (purpose text, sender account by `bankAccountId`, confirmed symbol by `symbolClaimId`, receipt number of the active single receipt, `receiptAvailable`), the issued receipts, the kept images and `agrees`; `summarizeDonations` adds up gifts as the server's summary does; `activeReceiptOf`, `imageDigestsOf`) and `copy-receipt-source.ts` (`copyBackedReceiptSource`: a receipt is issued online as before, and only when the server cannot be reached (status 0, or a gateway status without a problem body) the copy's active receipt of that year or gift is drawn; an image the copy keeps is read from it, any other from the server; the PDF is drawn again each time and never stored), `donor-donations-repository.ts` (`DonorDonationsRepository`, the one read of the donor's gifts for the pages, Donor pages below) and `testing/fake-donor-copy.ts` (`FakeDonorCopy` and `copyViewOf` for the page specs).
- `core/drafts/` (plan v3, 2.20e; how it is secured: `docs/security-overview.md`, "Device copies"): unsaved forms kept on the device. `draft-shelf.ts` (`DraftShelf` over `DeviceVault`: `'administration'` keeps into the open administration copy, `'donor'` into the account's own copy; `holder(copy)` names the copy id open and stored now, or none, so memory-only mode, "Cizí počítač" and a locked administration keep nothing, and every `list`, `keep` and `drop` names the copy id it means and is refused for any other; tokens `DRAFT_SHELF` and `DRAFT_KEEP_DELAY_MS`, 1 s) and `form-draft.ts` (`formDraft({ copy, key, values, apply, changedFieldsOnly? })`, a `FormDraft` per form: `ready(version?)` once the form shows what is saved, the base being `version:<v>` of a row version (2.18) or, for a record without one, `values:` and the SHA-256 of the stable JSON of what the form shows; edits are noticed from the host's `input` and `change` events (capture) or `changed()`; the first is kept at once (after the event, so the model has it), later ones at most once per `DRAFT_KEEP_DELAY_MS`, and the waiting one at once when the page is hidden, left or the component goes (an unload can still cut that last write short); an edit made before the base of a record without a version is digested waits for it; the draft is bound to the account signed in when the form started and to the copy id it first wrote into, `DraftShelf.holder(copy, account)` names a copy only when it is that account's and the session is still that account's, and when the tab turns to another account the `FormDraft` forgets what it holds, keeps nothing more and drops no draft of the other account, so one account's form never writes into another account's copy; an edit made while no copy of the account is open is kept as soon as one opens; Zahodit on an offer keeps what was typed before the offer showed, and leaving the page without answering keeps the offered draft; `changedFieldsOnly` keeps only the fields that differ from what the form showed and puts them back over what is saved now, so a stale draft of the settings or the profile brings back no field nobody touched; an edit back to what is saved drops the draft; `offer()` (`savedAt`, `stale` when the draft's base is not the current one), `resume()`, `discard()`, `saved(version?)`, `rebased(version)` (the record moved to a new version by the page's own unpublish with edits unsaved: the draft stays, under the new version), `left()`, `resumedStale()`, `staleVersion()` and `overwriteConfirmed()`; while an offer waits, nothing is kept and the page makes its form `inert` until the choice, so the offered draft stays as it was and nothing typed meanwhile is lost). `testing/memory-draft-shelf.ts` (`MemoryDraftShelf`, `memoryDraftShelfProviders`, `valuesBase`). `shared/ui/draft-offer/` (`<app-draft-offer>`, a region named by its own `h2` or `h3`: "Máte tu rozepsané úpravy", when they were made and to choose before editing on, that the saved version changed since when it is stale, that photos are not kept where the form has one, that passwords and tokens are not kept where it has them (`secrets`), "Pokračovat v rozepsaném" and "Zahodit"; `DRAFT_RESUMED_TEXT` and `DRAFT_DISCARDED_TEXT` for the page's status line). The forms: the news and project editors (`news/<id>` or `news/new`, `projects/…`, versioned; a stale draft resumed sends the version it was read at, so the server's 409 shows "Mezitím se to změnilo" with the lead `fromDraft`), the founders note and the line under the board (`people/note`, `people/footnote`), the operations settings (`settings`, every value but the secrets), the monthly report's settings (`report-settings`, versioned) and the donor's data on the receipt (`profile`, copy `donor`); for the unversioned ones a stale draft resumed asks with the same sheet (the profile with a warning in its form) before its first save. Not kept: the About photo and the backup plan (a photo and three choices, nothing typed), each person's editor in Lidé fondu, the contact form and the sign-in and registration forms.
- `core/seo/canonical-link.ts` (above) and `core/auth/`: `donor.ts` (DTOs `Donor`, `RegisterDonorRequest`, `AuthResponse`, `PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH` 12 shared by registration and reset), `auth-api.ts` (`/api/donors/auth/{register,login,login/second-factor,login/second-factor/recovery,refresh,logout}`, `email-verification/{request,confirm}`, `password-reset/{request,confirm}`, `/api/donors/me`, `/api/donors/me/password`, `/api/donors/me/email-change`, `/api/donors/me/second-factor/{setup,confirm,disable}`, `describeClient` → `/api/donors/oauth/client`, `authorize` → `/api/donors/oauth/authorize`, `connections` and `disconnect` → `/api/donors/me/connections[/{id}]`; `passkeySignInOptions` and `signInWithPasskey` → `/api/donors/auth/passkey[/options]`, `passkeys`, `passkeyCreationOptions(confirmation)`, `addPasskey` and `removePasskey` → `/api/donors/me/passkeys[/options|/{id}]`, `removePassword` → `/api/donors/me/password/remove`, `passkeyRecoveryOptions` and `recoverWithPasskey` → `/api/donors/auth/password-reset/passkey[/options]` (DTOs `PasskeyCreationOptions`, `PasskeySignInOptions`, `PasskeyRegistrationCredential`, `PasskeyAssertionCredential`, `Passkey`); the DTOs `ClientDescription`, `AuthorizationRequest`, `RedirectResponse`, `ConnectedApp`, `OAuthScope` in `donor.ts`), `auth-store.ts` (signals `donor`, `status`, `signedIn`, `isAdmin` (the session's roles contain `ADMIN`, which the backend grants only after the second factor); `fromDevice` (the account shows from the device copy without the server: a remembered session of the last 14 days whose restore found the app offline or the server unreachable, `copy-profile.ts`, `docs/security-overview.md` "Offline sign-in";
